Petition Under Article 226 of the Constrtution of lndia praying that in the
circumstances stated in the affidavit filed therewith, the High Court may be
pleased to issue a writ, order or direction, more particularly one in the nature of
WRIT OF MANDAMUS consequently direct the amendment to Section 11 dated
2g-O8-2O14 in GSR 609 (E) dated 22-08-2014 is contrary to'the judgments of
Kerala High Court and Apex Court in W.P.No.6643 ot 2OO7, W.A.No.568 of 2012
S.L.P.No.7076 of 2014 as unjust, illegal, arbitrary and violative of Articles 14, 1 6
and 21 ot the Constitution to declare that the Petitioners are entitled to get
pension on the basis of pensionable salary reckoned in accordance with their
,

actual salary from the date of their joining the Pension Scheme under the
Employees Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act 1952to direct the
respondents Nos. 2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension.
l.A.NO:1 OF 2018
Petition Under Section 15'1 CPC praying that in the circumstances stated
in the affidavit filed in support of the petition, the High Court may be pleased to
direct the Respondents No.2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension reckoning the
actual salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect on their appltcation
.lointly with respondent No.5 i.e., Coromandel lnternational Limited and accepting
from the Petitioners, the excess amount of the contribution which ought to have
been paid to the Pension Fund as contribution calculating the same on the basis
of their actual salary ignoring the amendment of 2014 pending the disposal of

. Petition Under Articte 226 of lhe Constitution of lndia praying that in the
circumstances stated in the affidavit filed therewith' the High Court may be
pleased to issue a writ, order or direction' more particularly one in the nature of
WRIT OF MANDAMUS to declare that l the amendment to Section 11 daled 29-
08-2014 in GSR 609 E dated 2?-O8-2O14 is contrary to the judgments of Kerala
High Court and Apex Court in W P No 6643 ot 2007 ' W A'No 568 ol 2012 and
S.L.P-No.7076 of 2O14 as unjust, illegal' arbitrary and violative of Articles 14' 16
and 21 of the Constitution of lndia 2' to declare that the Petitioners are entitled
to get pension on the basis of pensionable salary reckoned in accordince with
their actual salary from the date of their joining the Pension Scheme under the
Employees Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act 1952 3' to direct
the respondents Nos 2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension redoning their actual
salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect accepting from the
Petitioners the excess amount of contrlbution which they ought to have been
paid to the Pension Fund contributing the same on the basis of their actual salary
on their loint application with the respondenl No'5' ignoring the amendment of
20l4whichisliabletobedeclaredasillegal,unconstitutionalandviolationofall
principles of natural .iustice.
I.A.N :1 OF 2018
Petition Under Section 151 CPC praying that in the circumstances stated
in the affidavit filed in support of the petition, the High court may be pleased to
direct the Respondents Nos 2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension reckoning the
actualsalaryaspensionablesalarywithretrospectiveeffectontheirapplication
jointly with respondent No.S ie, The AP State Seed Development Corporation
Ltd and accepting from the Petitioners, the excess amount of the contribution
, I

calculating the same on the basis of their actual salary ignoring the amendment
of 2014 pending the disposal of writ petition.

...RESPONDENTS
Petition Under Atlicle 226 of the Constitution of lndia praying that in the
circumstances stated in the affidavit filed therewith, the High Court may be
pleased to issue a writ, order or direction, more particularly one in the nature of
WRIT OF MANDAMUS to declare that (i) the amendment to Section 11 dated 29-
08-2014 in GSR 609 (E) dated 22-08-2014 is contrary to the judgments of Kerala
High Court and Apex Court in W.P.No.6643 of 2007, W.A.No.568 of 2012 and
S.L.P.No.7076 of 2O14 as.unjust, illegal, arbitrary and violative of Articles 14, 16
and 21 ot the constitution of lndia. (ii) to declare that the Petitioners are entitled
to get pension on the basis of pensionable salary reckoned in accordance with
their actual salary from the date of their joining the Pension Scheme under the
Employees Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act 1952- (iii) to direct
the respondents Nos.2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension reckoning their actual
salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect accepting from the
Petitioners the excess amount of contribution which they ought to have been
paid to the Pension Fund contributing the same on the basis of their aStual salary
on their joint applications with the respondents No.5 , 7 ignoring the amendment
of 2014 which is liable to be declared as illegal, unconstitutiona I and violation of
all principles of natural justice. I
lA NO: 1 OF 2018
Petition under Section 151 CPC praying that in the circumstances stated
in the affidavit filed in support of the petition, the High Court may be pleased to
direct the Respondents Nos. 2 to 4 to pay the petitioners pension reckoning the
actual salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect on their application
iointly with respondents Nos. 5 and 7 and accepting from the Petitioners' the
excess amount of the contribution which ought to have been paid to the Pension
Fund as contribution calculating the same on the basis of their aclual salary
ignoring the amendment of 2014 pending the disposal of writ petition.
Counsel for the Petitioners : SRI B.ANJANEYULU
Counsel for the Respondent Nos.I & 2 : SRI GADI PRAVEEN KUMAR, DEPUTY
SOLICITOR GENERAL OF INDIA

,..RESPONDENTS

Petition under A icle 226 of the Constitution of lndia praying that in the
circumstances stated in the affidavit filed therewith, the High Court may be pleased
toto issue a writ, order or direction, more particularly one in the nature of Writ of
Mandamus consequently direct i. the amendment to Section 11 dated 29-08-2014
in GSR 609 (E) dated 22-OB 2014 is contrary to the judgments of Kerala High Court
and Apex court in w.P.No.6643 of 2OO7, W.A.No.568 ol 2012 and s.L.P.No.7076
of 2014 as unjust, illegal, arbitrary and violative of Articles 14, 16 and 21 of the
Constitution of lndia. ii. to declare that the Petitioners are entitled to get pension on
the basis of pensionable salary reckoned in accordance with their actual salary
from the date of their joining the Pension Scheme under the Employees Provident
Funds and Miscellaneous.Provisions Act 1952. iii. to direct the respondents Nos. 2
to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension reckoning their actual salary as pensionable
salary with retrospective effect accepting from the Petitioners the excess amount of
contribution which they ought to have been paid to the Pension Fund contributing
the same on the basis of their actual salary on their joint application with the
respondent No.5, ignoring the amendment of 2014 which is liable to be declared as
illegal, unconstitutional and violation of all principles of natural justice.
lA NO: 1 OF 2018
Petition under Section 151 CPC praying that in the circumstances stated in
the affidavit filed in support of the petition, the High Court may be pleasqd to direct
the Respondents Nos. 2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension reckoning' the actual
salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect on their application jointly
with respondent No.5 i.e., Coromandel lnternational Limited and accepting from the
Petitioners, the excess.amount of the contribution which ought to have been paid to
the Pension Fund as contribution calculating the same on thelasis of their actual
salary ignoring the amendmenl ot 2014 pending the disposal'of writ petition and
pass

Petition under Article 226 of the Constitution of lndia prayrng that in the

2014 in GSR 609 (E) dated 22-08 2014 is contrary to the lHdgments of Kerata

accordance with their actual salaiy from the date of their joining the pensron

Petition Under Article 226 of the Constitution of India praying that in the
circumstances stated in the affidavit filed therewith, the High Court may be
pleased to issue a writ, order or direction, more particularly one in the nature of
Writ Of lvlandamus to declare that (i) the amendment to Section 11 dated 29-08-
2014 in GSR 609 (E) daled 22-Oa-2014 is contrary to the judgments of Kerala
High Court ancJ Apex Court in W.P.No.6643 of 2007, W A.No 569 of 2O12 and
a

S.L.P.No.7070- of 2014 as unjust, illegal, arbitrary and violative of Articles 14, 16
and 21 of the constitution of lndia. (ii) to declare that the Petitioners are entitled
lo get pension on the basis of pensionable salary reckoned in accordance with
their actual salary from the dale of their joining the Pension Scheme under the
Employees Provident Funds and lritiscellaneous Provisions Act 1952. (iii) to direct
the respondenls Nos. 2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension reckoning their actual
salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect accepting from the
Petitioners the excess amount of contribution which they ought to have been
paid to the Pension Fund contributing the same on the basis of their actual salary
on their joint application with the respondent No.5, ignoring the amendment of
2014 which is liable to be declared as illegal, unconstitutional and violation of all
principles of natural justice.

Petition under Article 226 of the Constitution of lndizr praying that in the
circumstances stated in the affidavit filed therewith, the High Court may be
pleased toto Honble Court may be pleased to issue a wri:, order or direction,
more particularly one in the nature of Writ of Mandamus rlonsequent[y direct (i)
the amendment to Section 11 dated 29-08-2014 in GSR €09 (E) dated 22-08-
2014 is contrary to the judgments of Kerala High Court and Apex Court in
W.P.No.6643 of 2007, W.A.No.568 of 2O12 and S L.P.No 707$of 2014 as unjust,
illegal, arbitrary and violative of Articles 14, 16 and 21 cf the Constitution of
lndia. (ii) to declare that the Petitioners are entitled to get pension on the basis of
pensionable salary reckoned in accordance with thetr actual salary from the date
of their joining the Pension Scheme under the Employees Provident Funds and
Miscellaneous Provisions Act 1952. (iii) to direct the respondents Nos 2 to 4 to
pay the Petitioners pension reckoning their actual salary as pensionable salary
with retrospective effect accepting from the Petitioners the excess amount of
contribution which they ought to have been paid to the Pension Fund contributing
the same on the basis of their actual salary on their joirt application with the
respondent No.5, ignoring lhe amendmenl ol 2014 which i:; liable to be declared
as illegal, unconstitutional and violation of all principles of rratural justice.

direct the Respondents Nos. 2 to 4 to pay the Petitloners pension reckoning the

actual salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect on their application

.....RESPONDENTS
Petition Under Article 226 of the Constitution of lndia praying that in the
circumstances stated in the affidavit filed therewith, the High Court may be
pleased to issue a writ, order or direction, more particularly one in the nature of
Writ of Mandamus consequently direct 1. the amendment to Section 11 dated 29-
O8-2O14 in GSR 609 (E) dated 22-08-2014 is conlrary to the judgments of Kerala
High Courl and Apex Court in W.P.No.6643 of 2007, W A.No.568 of 2012 and
S-L.P.No.7076 of 2014 as unjust, illegal, arbitrary and violative of Articles 14, 16
and 21 of the Constitution of lndia.2. to declare that the Petitioners are entitled to
get pension on the basis of pensionable salary reckoned in accordance with their
actual salary from the date of their joining the Pension Scheme under the

Employees Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act 1952.3. to direct
the respondents Nos. 2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension reckoning their actual
salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect accepting from the
Petitioners the excess amount of contribution which they ought to have been
paid to the Pension Fund contributing the same on the basis of their actual salary
on their joint application with the respondent No 5, ignoring the amendment of
2014 which is liable to be declared as illegal, unconstitutional and violation of all
principles of natural .justice.ln the circumstances explained, the Petitioners pray
the Honorable Court may be pleased to direct the Respondent Nos 2 to 4 to pay
the Petitioners pension reckoning the actual salary as pensionable salary with
retrospective effect on their application .iointly with respondent No 5 ie, Food
corporation of lndia and accepting from the Petitioners, the excess amount of
the contribution calculating the same on the basis of their actual salary ignoring
the amendment of 2014 pending the disposal of writ petition'
IA NOz 1 OF 2017
PetitionUndersectionl5lCPCprayingthatinthecircumstancesstaled
in the affidavit frled in support of the petition, the High court may be pleased to
direct the Respondent Nos. 2 to 4 to pay the Petitioners pension reckoning the
actual salary as pensionable salary with retrospective effect6n their application
.lointly with respondent No.5 ie, Food corporation of lndia and accepting from the
Petitioners, the excess amount of the contribution calculating the same on the
basis of their actual salary ignoring the amendment of 2014 pending the disposal
of writ petition.

The Court made the following COMMON ORDER
tl

THE HON'BLE Szu JUSTICE P.SAM KOSHY
AND
THE HON'BLE Szu JUSTICE NARSING RAO NANDIKONDA
WRIT PETITION Nos.40733 221 306 1678 3145 3234 20076 31427 of
W.P.Nos.44091 45294 45295 of 2017 W.P.Nos .8852 & 16317 of2019 2018
COMMON ORDER:(Nr lton'blc Sri Jksttcc P.SALI I(OSHY)
Heard leamed counsel for the petitioners, Mr'Narsimha Sharma,
Additional Solicitor General of India assisted by Ms.L.Pranathi Reddy,
leamed counsel appearing fbr respondent No' l, Mr'M'P'Durga Prasad,
leamed counsel appearing for respondent No.6, Mr.Madhu Sudhan Reddy,
leamed counsel appearing for respondent Nos.2 to 4 and Mr'G'V'S'Ganesh,
leamed counsel appearing for respondent No.5 in W.P.No22l of 2018'
2. The challenge in this batch of writ petitions is to the amendment to the
section l1 in GSR 609(E), dated22.08.2A14. The challenge was that the
said GSR was violative of Article Nos.14, 16 and 2l of the constitution of
India and also contrary to the existing pensionery benefits that the
petitioners were drawing.
3.Today,whenthematteristakenupforhearing,ithasbeenbroughtto
the notice of this Bench that identical matters had travelled to the Hon'ble
Supreme court lrom the High court of Kerala, Rajasthan and from Delhi
I

7
2
High Court and the batch of Special Leave Petitions decided vide judgment
dated 04.11.2022.The said judgment stands reported in(2(123) I2 SCC 701'
4. All the counscls representing the respective respondents have fairly
accepted the fact that the^present batch of writ petitions zLlso being identical
in nature can be disposed of in terms of the guidelines so laid down by the
Hon'ble Supreme Court while deciding the aforesaid batch of SLPs. In the
said batch of Sl,Ps, the Hon'ble Supreme Court after extensively
considering the matter while disposing of the said SLPs have held as under:
''We accordingly hold and direct:
(i) The provisions contained in the notification no. G.S.R.609(E)
dated 22nd August 2014 are legal and valid. So far dt present
rnembers of the fund are concemed, we have read dowm certain
provisions of the scheme as applicable in their cases and we shall
give our l'indings and directions on thcse provisions in the
subsequcnt subparagraphs.
(ii) Amendmenl to the pension scheme brought about by the
notification no. G.S.R.609(E) dated 22 nd August 20t4 shall apply
to the employees of the exempted establishrnents in the same manner
as the emplol'ees of the regular establishments. Transfer of funds
from the exempted establishments shall be in the manncr as we have
already directed.
(iii) The employces rvho had exercised option under thc proviso to
paragraph ll(3) of the 1995 scheme and continued to be in service
as on lst September 2014, wilt be guided by the amended provisions
ofparagraph I 1(4) ofthe pension scheme.
(iv) 'fhe members ol'the scheme, who did not exetcrse option, as
contemplated in the proviso to paragraph I l(3) ol the pension
scheme (as it rvas belbre the 2014 Amendment) would bc entitled to
exercise option under paragraph I l(a) of thc post amendment
scheme. Their righl to exercise option before Ist Seotember 2014
stands crJ stalised in the jrldgqent of this'Coun in the case ol !!Q.
(iupla (supra). The scheme as it stood before lst Septernber 2014 did

.3 I{
not provide for any cut off date and thus those members shall be
entitled to exercise option in terms of paragraphl I (4) of the scheme,
as it stands at present. Their exercise of option shall be in the nature
ofjoint options covering pre-amended paragraph I l(3) as also the
amended paragraph I t(4) ofthe pension scheme.
There was uncertainty as regards validity of the post amendment
scheme, which was quashed by the aforesaid judgments of the three
High Courts. Thus, a[[ the employees who did not exercise option
but were entitled to do so but could not due to the interpretation on
cutoff date by the authorities, ought to be given a further chance to
exercise their option. Time to cxercise option under paragraph I l(4)
of the scheme, under these circumstanccs, shall stand extended by
a
further period of four months. We are giving this direction in
exercise of our jurisdiction under Article 142 of the Constitution of
India.
Rest of the requirements as per the amended provision shall be
complied with. .
(v) The employees who had retired prior to I st September 2014
without exercising any option under paragraph l1(3) of the pre-amendment scheme have already exited from the membership
thereof. They would not be entitled to the benefit of this ju$ment.
(vi) The employees who havc retired before lst September 2014
upon exercising option under paragraph ll(3) of the 1995 scheme
shall be covered by the provisions of the paragraph ll(3) of the
pension scheme as it stood prior to thc amendment of 2014.
(vii) The requircment of the members to contribute at the rate of 1 . l6
per cent of their salary to the extent such salary exceeds Rs.15000/
per month as an additional contribution under the amended scheme
is hetd to be ultra vires the provisions of the 1952 Act. But for the
reasons already explained above, we suspend operation of this part
of our order for a period of six months. We do so to enable the
authorities to make adjustments in the scheme so that the additional
contribution can be generated from some other legitimate source
within the scope of the Act, which could include enhancing the rate
of contribution of the employers. We are not speculating on what
steps the authorities will take as it would be for the legislature or the
framers of the scheme to make necessary amendment. For the
aforesaid period ol six months or till such time any amendment is
made, whichever is earlier, the employces' contribution shall be as
stop gap measure.
The said sum shall be adjustable on the basis of alteration to the
scheme that may be made.

(viii) We do not find any flaw in altering the basis for compurarion
of pensionable salary.
